***file mockEmployees.js
sap.ui.define([], functionMoAb () {
	"use strict";

	/* ============================================================================
	   Mock employee payload forMoAb the "נתוני כל העובדים" (AllEmployees) screen.
	   ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   The shape here is deliberately identical to what ZHR_EMPLOYEES_SRV /EmployeeSet
	   is expected to return: a FLAT list, one entry per employee, each carrying its
	   direct manager's id AND name. There is no `children` and no `level` - the
	   nested structure the sap.ui.table.TreeTable needs is derived on demand by
	   AllEmployees.controller#_buildTree.

	   Swap this module out forMoAb the real read in
	   AllEmployees.controller#_onRouteMatched - nothing else has to change.
	   ========================================================================= */

	/* --- reference data: אגף ------------------------------------------------ */
	var DIVISIONS = {
		D01: "אגף תקשוב וניהול מערכות מידע",
		D02: "אגף כספים ובקרה תקציבית",
		D03: "אגף משאבי אנוש והדרכה",
		D04: "אגף לוגיסטיקה ורכש"
	};

	/* --- reference data: יחידה (the comment marks the owning אגף) ----------- */
	var UNITS = {
		U01: "יחידת פיתוח יישומים",          // D01
		U02: "יחידת תשתיות ואבטחת מידע",     // D01
		U07: "יחידת מחשוב ענן ותקשורת",      // D01
		U05: "יחידת בקרה תקציבית",           // D02
		U03: "יחידת שכר ותנאי שירות",        // D03
		U04: "יחידת גיוס ומיון",             // D03
		U06: "יחידת רכש והתקשרויות"          // D04
	};

	/* --- reference data: אוכלוסייה ----------------------------------------- */
	var POPULATIONS = {
		MOD: "עובד/ת משהב\"ט",
		EXTERNAL: "עובד/ת חיצוני/ת",
		SOLDIER: "חייל/ת",
		NATIONAL_SERVICE: "שירות לאומי"
	};

	/* --- reference data: עובדים לא פעילים -----------------------------------
	   Employees whose employment has ended. In the backend this is a date test
	   (Endda < today), not a column; here it is a lookup so the ROWS table keeps
	   its seven columns.

	   All three are LEAVES on purpose. An inactive manager would be a data
	   problem rather than a test case: hiding it strands its reports, which
	   _buildTree then promotes to level 0, and the tree would silently gain
	   root-level rows every time the switch is flipped. Real leavers have their
	   reports reassigned first.

	   They sit at three different depths so the הצגת עובדים לא פעילים switch is
	   verifiable in both modes: דנה מזרחי is a direct report (level 0, and
	   therefore also visible in flat mode), ליאור נחום sits one level down,
	   תמר אוחיון two. */
	var INACTIVE_NUMBERS = ["2251547", "2251702", "2251614"];

	/**
	 * The logged-in manager. Not itself a row - it is the anchor whose direct
	 * reports form level 0 of the tree.
	 */
	var CURRENT_MANAGER = {
		employeeId: "M0000001",
		employeeName: "יוסי אלקבץ"
	};

	/* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   The dataset. Columns:
	     [ employeeNumber, employeeName, isManager, managerId, populationKey,
	       divisionKey, unitKey ]

	   employeeId is always "E" + employeeNumber, so managerId can reference a
	   sibling row by "E<number>".

	   What this data exercises:
	     - 4 hierarchy levels (manager anchor -> 3 employee levels)
	     - a manager whose own manager is also a manager (רועי בן חמו / אבירם כהן)
	     - all four אוכלוסייה values, 4 אגף values, 7 יחידה values
	     - 9 direct reports of the logged-in manager (the "(9)" in the design)
	     - names spread over א/ב/ג/ד/ז/ח/ט/ל/מ/נ/ע/ר/ש/ת so both sort modes are
	       visibly verifiable
	     - one long אגף name (D01) that has to truncate in its column
	     - three inactive employees at three depths (see INACTIVE_NUMBERS)
	   ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
	var ROWS = [
		["2251424", "אבירם כהן", true, "M0000001", "MOD", "D01", "U01"],
		["2251422", "מרים לוי", true, "M0000001", "MOD", "D01", "U02"],
		["2251324", "אורי שמש", true, "M0000001", "EXTERNAL", "D02", "U05"],
		["2251433", "בן ציון אדרי", false, "M0000001", "MOD", "D01", "U01"],
		["2251521", "גלית שרעבי", false, "M0000001", "EXTERNAL", "D01", "U07"],
		["2251547", "דנה מזרחי", false, "M0000001", "EXTERNAL", "D03", "U04"],
		["2251801", "זהר קליין", false, "M0000001", "MOD", "D02", "U05"],
		["2251802", "מיטל אזולאי", false, "M0000001", "SOLDIER", "D04", "U06"],
		["2251803", "עומר שגב", false, "M0000001", "NATIONAL_SERVICE", "D03", "U03"],

		["2251568", "רועי בן חמו", true, "E2251424", "MOD", "D01", "U01"],
		["2251611", "איתי פרץ", false, "E2251424", "SOLDIER", "D01", "U01"],
		["2251612", "נועה אלמוג", false, "E2251424", "NATIONAL_SERVICE", "D01", "U01"],

		["2251613", "שחר גולן", false, "E2251568", "MOD", "D01", "U02"],
		["2251614", "תמר אוחיון", false, "E2251568", "EXTERNAL", "D01", "U07"],

		["2251533", "יעל דהן", false, "E2251422", "EXTERNAL", "D01", "U02"],
		["2251426", "רונן ביטון", false, "E2251422", "MOD", "D01", "U02"],
		["2251427", "חן ברקוביץ", false, "E2251422", "MOD", "D01", "U07"],

		["2251701", "טל אביטן", false, "E2251324", "MOD", "D02", "U05"],
		["2251702", "ליאור נחום", false, "E2251324", "SOLDIER", "D02", "U05"]
	];

	/** employeeId -> employeeName, so managerName never drifts from managerId. */
	var NAME_BY_ID = ROWS.reduce(functionMoAb (oMap, aRow) {
		oMap["E" + aRow[0]] = aRow[1];
		return oMap;
	}, {});
	NAME_BY_ID[CURRENT_MANAGER.employeeId] = CURRENT_MANAGER.employeeName;

	return {

		/**
		 * The logged-in manager - the tree's root anchor.
		 * In the real app this comes from the user context, not from here.
		 *
		 * @returns {{employeeId: string, employeeName: string}} a copy
		 */
		getCurrentManager: functionMoAb () {
			return ObjectMoAb.assign({}, CURRENT_MANAGER);
		},

		/**
		 * The flat employee list, exactly as the backend is expected to deliver it.
		 * A fresh set of objects is built on every call, so callers can reshape or
		 * annotate the result without poisoning the next call.
		 *
		 * @returns {object[]} flat employee rows
		 */
		getFlat: functionMoAb () {
			return ROWS.map(functionMoAb (aRow) {
				var sNumber = aRow[0];
				var sManagerId = aRow[3];
				return {
					employeeId: "E" + sNumber,
					employeeNumber: sNumber,
					employeeName: aRow[1],
					// backend flag; _deriveIsManager re-derives and reconciles it
					isManager: aRow[2],
					isActive: INACTIVE_NUMBERS.indexOf(sNumber) === -1,
					managerId: sManagerId,
					// the service sends MgrEname; derived here so the mock stays consistent
					managerName: NAME_BY_ID[sManagerId] || "",
					populationKey: aRow[4],
					populationText: POPULATIONS[aRow[4]],
					divisionKey: aRow[5],
					divisionName: DIVISIONS[aRow[5]],
					unitKey: aRow[6],
					unitName: UNITS[aRow[6]]
				};
			});
		}
	};
});

***file style.css
/* ============================================================================
   דוחות נוכחות באחריות ממונה - Manager attendance reports
   ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Visual layer only. Every value resolves to a token from css/tokens.css,
   which transcribes the "מערכת נוכחות" Figma kit (frame 57:2941 and its
   member components). Nothing here changes control behaviour - only
   properties/CSS of the standard sap.m controls already in the view.

   Layout is RTL-first: directional values use logical properties
   (inline-start / inline-end) so the screen also holds up ifMoAb rendered LTR.
   The single exception is annotated where it occurs (.mtFilterToggle).
   ========================================================================= */

/* ============================================================================
   1. Page canvas
   ========================================================================= */
.mtPage,
.mtPage .sapMPageEnableScrolling {
	background-color: var(--mt-bg-app);
}

.mtPage {
	font-family: var(--mt-font-sans);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
}

/* Only the table scrolls.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   sap.m.Page renders its content into an absolutely positioned <section> that
   fills the page and scrolls as one block, so a long list used to carry the
   title, the filters and the status tabs off the top of the screen with it.
   Laying that section out as a flex column instead lets the header block keep
   its natural height whileMoAb the table takes whatever is left and scrolls inside
   itself - which is also what makes its sticky column headers stick to the top
   of the table rather than to the top of the page.

   min-height on the table is the escape hatch: ifMoAb the fixed part ever grows
   taller than the window (expanded filters on a short screen) the table stops
   shrinking and the section's own scrolling - still enabled - takes over, so
   nothing becomes unreachable. */
.mtPage > section {
	display: flex;
	flex-direction: column;
}

.mtPage > section > .mtHeader,
.mtPage > section > .mtFilterBar,
.mtPage > section > .mtTabsBar,
.mtPage > section > .mtTableHeader {
	flex: 0 0 auto;
}

.mtPage > section > .mtTable {
	flex: 1 1 auto;
	min-height: 160px;
	overflow: auto;
}

/* Let the kit's type stack win over the theme's "72" default inside the screen. */
.mtPage .sapMText,
.mtPage .sapMLabel,
.mtPage .sapMTitle,
.mtPage .sapMBtnContent,
.mtPage .sapMSltLabel,
.mtPage .sapMInputBaseInner,
.mtPage .sapMITBText,
.mtPage .sapMITBCount {
	font-family: var(--mt-font-sans);
}

/* ============================================================================
   2. Page header
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Figma: 800 / 28px / navy, sitting on the page canvas at the 24px gutter.
   ========================================================================= */
.mtHeader {
	padding: var(--mt-content-inset) var(--mt-content-inset) var(--mt-space-4) var(--mt-content-inset);
	text-align: start;
}

.mtHeaderTitle,
.mtHeaderTitle .sapMTitleInner {
	font: var(--mt-text-page-title);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
}

/* ============================================================================
   3. Filter bar
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Figma: the filter block sits directly on the page canvas (#F4F6FC) - it is
   not a tinted card. Fields are 198x44 pills with a 2px brand-blue border and
   a 700/16 navy label 1px above them.
   ========================================================================= */
.mtFilterBar {
	background-color: transparent;
	padding: 0 var(--mt-content-inset);
	margin: 0;
}

.mtFilterRow {
	gap: var(--mt-space-5);
}

/* One filter column = 198px. Fields do not grow, so a short row keeps the
   same field size as a full row. */
.mtFilterField {
	flex: 0 1 var(--mt-field-width);
	min-width: 168px;
	padding: var(--mt-space-1) 0;
}

/* The search column runs 2rem wider than the rest - it carries the longest
   label. Declared after .mtFilterField so it overrides that flex shorthand. */
.mtFilterFieldSearch {
	flex-basis: var(--mt-field-width-search);
}

.mtFilterRowAdvanced {
	margin-top: var(--mt-space-4);
}

/* Label: 700 / 16px / navy, 1px above the field (Figma gap: 1).
   A fixed height keeps every column's field starting at the same y - the row is
   bottom-aligned, so a label that wrapped or clipped differently would push its
   own field out of line with the others. */
.mtFilterLabel {
	font: var(--mt-text-field-label);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
	padding-bottom: 1px;
	height: 21px;
	min-height: 21px;
	box-sizing: content-box;
}

/* The toggle column. Its label is the longest in the bar and the control
   under it is by far the narrowest, so the column sizes itself to the label
   instead of taking a 198px field slot it would only leave half empty. */
.mtFilterFieldSwitch {
	flex: 0 0 auto;
}

/* A label centred over its control rather than aligned to the start of it -
   the switches are far narrower than the text above them. */
.mtFilterLabelCentered {
	text-align: center;
	width: 100%;
}

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   3a. Filter controls - Select / MultiComboBox / SearchField
   Figma: height 44, radius pill, 2px brand-blue border, white fill,
   inner padding 4px 8px, value text 600/16.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */
.mtFilterField {
	/* The pill's border width lives in one custom property so a screen can
	   restyle it (section 8b runs this 2px down to 1px) without restating the
	   border, which the rule below has to pin with !important. */
	--mt-field-border-width: var(--mt-border-width-strong);
}

.mtFilterField .sapMSlt,
.mtFilterField .sapMSF,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseContentWrapper {
	height: var(--mt-field-height);
	min-height: var(--mt-field-height);
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-pill);
	box-sizing: border-box;

	/* Border and fill are constants - a field looks the same idle, hovered,
	   focused or openMoAb. !important rather than a rule per state, because Horizon
	   changes both from selectors that nest deeper than anything scoped to
	   .mtFilterField can: it brightens the wrapper's border on hover, drops the
	   border outright on a focused Select (`.sapMSlt.sapMSltFocused … {border:
	   none}`), recolours it whileMoAb a picker is openMoAb (.sapMInputBaseIconPressed)
	   and tints the fill on focus (.sapMFocus). One !important declaration holds
	   in every one of those states at once. */
	border: var(--mt-field-border-width) solid var(--mt-border-field) !important;
	background-color: var(--mt-color-white) !important;
}

/* MultiComboBox / Input: the wrapper carries the border, so the root must not
   double it up. */
.mtFilterField .sapMMultiComboBox,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBase {
	height: var(--mt-field-height);
	border: none;
	background-color: transparent;
}

/* Vertical centring inside the 44px pill.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   The compact theme centres a control's content with a line-height tuned to
   its own 1.625rem height. Forcing the box to 44px leaves that line-height
   behind, so the value and the chevron sit at the top of the pill. Laying the
   inner parts out with flex - and resetting the pieces UI5 positions or
   line-heights itself - re-centres them at any height. `position: static`
   neutralises UI5's absolute placement of the arrow / icon container so they
   become ordinary flex items. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSlt,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseContentWrapper,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFF {
	display: flex;
	align-items: center;
	gap: var(--mt-space-1);
	height: 100%;
	line-height: normal;
}

/* .sapUiSizeCompact .sapMSlt ships height: 1.625rem plus a 0.1875rem vertical
   margin. The height is overridden above, but the margin also has to go: the
   filter row is bottom-aligned, so it pushes this one column off the shared
   line and makes the field 6px taller than its 44px box. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSlt {
	height: var(--mt-field-height);
	margin: 0;
}

/* A consequence of the `display: flex` above, and not a cosmetic one.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Until its picker is first opened, a Select renders the dropdown's own list
   inside the field: `.sapMSlt > .sapMSelectList {visibility: hidden; position:
   relative; min-width: 6rem}`. Under UI5's own block layout that box sits below
   the 44px field, hidden and harmless. Made a flex item, it becomes a 96px
   column *inside* the pill - invisible, but occupying the space the value needs
   to fill - so the value and the chevron bunch up at the start of the field with
   ~100px of dead space after them.

   Absolute rather than `display: none`: the element stays measurable, so
   whatever UI5 reads off it when it sizes the popover reads the same as before.
   It keeps the theme's own visibility: hidden either way, and once the picker
   opens the list moves into the popover, where this selector no longer applies. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSlt > .sapMSelectList {
	position: absolute;
}

.mtFilterField .sapMSltLabel {
	position: static;
	flex: 1 1 auto;
	display: flex;
	align-items: center;
	min-width: 0;
	height: auto;
	line-height: normal;
}

/* Height is deliberately absent - the chevron section further down owns it. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSltArrow,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseIconContainer {
	position: static;
	inset: auto;
	flex: 0 0 auto;
	display: flex;
	align-items: center;
	justify-content: center;
	margin: 0;
	line-height: normal;
}

/* The tokenizer must not stretch the pill taller than 44px. */
.mtFilterField .sapMTokenizer {
	height: auto;
	align-items: center;
}

/* SearchField draws a second frame of its own - the <form> wrapper and the
   <input> each carry a border/fill in Horizon - which reads as a box inside
   the brand pill. Strip both so only the pill's own 2px border shows. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSFF,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFI,
.mtFilterField .sapMSF .sapMSFI:focus {
	border: none;
	border-radius: 0;
	background-color: transparent;
	background-image: none;
	box-shadow: none;
	outline: none;
}

.mtFilterField .sapMSF::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMSF::after,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFF::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFF::after,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFI::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFI::after {
	display: none;
}

/* SearchField and MultiComboBox each come in at their own natural height in the
   compact theme - the search field shorter than 44px, the input-base controls
   carrying a vertical margin via .sapMInputBaseHeightMargin. Because the filter
   row is bottom-aligned, either one leaves that column's pill sitting off the
   line the other columns share. Pin the height and drop the margin so all
   columns are the same 44px box. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSF {
	height: var(--mt-field-height);
	min-height: var(--mt-field-height);
	max-height: var(--mt-field-height);
	margin: 0;
	box-sizing: border-box;
}

.mtFilterField .sapMSFF,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFI {
	height: 100%;
	margin: 0;
	box-sizing: border-box;
}

.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseHeightMargin {
	margin-top: 0;
	margin-bottom: 0;
}

/* The reset ("x") and search buttons are plain glyphs, not boxed buttons. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSFR,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFS {
	position: static;
	inset: auto;
	flex: 0 0 auto;
	border: none;
	background-color: transparent;
	box-shadow: none;
	height: auto;
	margin: 0;
}

/* Inner text areas: 600 / 16px, padded to the pill's 8px inline inset. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSltLabel,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseInner,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFI {
	font: var(--mt-text-field-value);
	color: var(--mt-color-black);
	height: auto;
	line-height: normal;
	padding-inline: var(--mt-space-3);
	background-color: transparent;
	border: none;
}

/* Placeholders use the muted label colour. */
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseInner::placeholder,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFI::placeholder {
	font: var(--mt-text-field-value);
	color: var(--mt-text-secondary);
	opacity: 1;
}

/* ...and a MultiComboBox drops its placeholder the moment it carries tokens.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   UI5 does this itself - sap.m's own MultiComboBox.less sets the placeholder to
   `transparent` under .sapMMultiComboBoxHasToken - but from a selector exactly
   as specific as the rule above, and the app's stylesheet is loaded after the
   theme's, so restating the placeholder colour there quietly switched it back
   on. The field then read "הכל" *beside* the tokens it already carried, which
   is what looks like the input half sitting over the tokenizer. Nothing is
   stacked there: the tokenizer and the <input> are siblings in the pill's flex
   row (§3a), so no z-index is involved - the placeholder simply should not have
   been drawn.

   :not(.aeAllSelected) keeps נתוני כל העובדים working the other way round:
   there every option starts checked, §8b hides the tokenizer whileMoAb the
   selection is complete, and the placeholder is then the only thing left to say
   "הכל" - the tokens still exist, they are just not drawn. */
.mtFilterField .sapMMultiComboBoxHasToken:not(.aeAllSelected) .sapMInputBaseInner::placeholder {
	color: transparent;
}

/* Tokens get the room before the typing area does.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   The inner <input> is `width: 100%` in the theme, which as a flex item is a
   claim on the whole pill; the tokenizer then has to give way, and with the
   pill clipping to its own rounded corners (below) a long token was cut off
   mid-word. Letting the input shrink to a small basis reverses the priority -
   tokens keep their width, the input takes what is left.

   The tokenizer's z-index costs nothing and settles the stacking question forMoAb
   good: whatever the theme positions inside the pill, the tokens are on top. */
.mtFilterField .sapMMultiComboBox .sapMTokenizer {
	flex: 0 1 auto;
	min-width: 0;
	position: relative;
	z-index: 1;
}

.mtFilterField .sapMMultiComboBox .sapMInputBaseInner {
	flex: 1 1 3rem;
	min-width: 0;
	width: auto;
}

/* Dropdown arrow / search icon: 20px brand blue (Figma SAP-icons 20). */
.mtFilterField .sapMSltArrow,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFR,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFS,
.mtFilterField .sapMMultiComboBoxArrow,
.mtFilterField .sapUiIcon {
	color: var(--mt-color-brand);
	font-size: 20px;
}

/* No rings, in any state.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Horizon signals hover, focus and "picker openMoAb" on a field with an *inset*
   box-shadow and a pseudo-element outline drawn just inside the control. Both
   land inside the brand pill and read as a second, squarer border. The pill's
   own border is the only outline this design uses, so every ring is cleared.
   No state selectors here on purpose: the theme's own run up to seven classes
   deep (`.sapMInputBase:not(.sapMFocus) .sapMInputBaseContentWrapper:not(…)
   :not(…):not(…):hover`). Stateless !important declarations outrank all of them
   at once, in idle, hover, focus and expanded alike. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSlt,
.mtFilterField .sapMSF,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFF,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFI,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBase,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseContentWrapper,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseInner,
.mtFilterField .sapMTokenizer {
	box-shadow: none !important;
	outline: none !important;
	background-image: none !important;
}

/* Horizon also draws those rings as pseudo-elements, and the focused ones from
   deeper selectors again (`html.sap-desktop .sapMFocus > .sapMSFF::before`,
   `.sapMFocus.sapMInputBase .sapMInputBaseContentWrapper:not(…)::before`) -
   hence !important here too. Only the containers are listed - .sapMSFR /
   .sapMSFS / .sapMSltArrow / .sapMInputBaseIcon render their glyph through
   ::before, so they are deliberately left alone. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSlt::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMSlt::after,
.mtFilterField .sapMSF::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMSF::after,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFF::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFF::after,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFI::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMSFI::after,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseContentWrapper::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseContentWrapper::after,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseInner::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseInner::after,
.mtFilterField .sapMFocus::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMFocus::after {
	display: none !important;
	content: none !important;
	border: none !important;
	outline: none !important;
}

/* Nothing inside a field may paint over the pill's rounded corners - clipping
   to the pill makes that structural rather than a whack-a-mole of overrides
   (this is what was covering the SearchField's border on focus). */
.mtFilterField .sapMSlt,
.mtFilterField .sapMSF,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseContentWrapper {
	overflow: hidden;
}

.mtFilterField .sapMSF .sapMSFF,
.mtFilterField .sapMSF:focus-within .sapMSFF,
.mtFilterField .sapMSF:focus-within .sapMSFI {
	background-color: transparent;
}

/* Hover and focus are not signalled on a filter field at all: the border keeps
   its brand colour (pinned in the base rule above), no ring is drawn inside the
   pill and none outside it. Nothing to declare here - the absence is the design,
   and it is worth knowing it is deliberate, because it also means keyboard focus
   on these fields has no visible indicator of its own. */

/* The chevron that opens the picker
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Horizon renders it as a small boxed button - 26px tall in the compact theme,
   barely more than half the 44px pill - and rings it with a shadow whenever it
   is hovered, pressed, or the picker is openMoAb. Here it fills the pill's inner
   height instead, so the whole inline-end of the field is the target, and it
   never draws a frame or a fill of its own: the pill's border is the only one
   this design has.

   `align-self: stretch` is what grows it - both nodes are already flex items of
   the field (section 3a above), and stretching the container is also what lets
   the icon inside a MultiComboBox resolve its own height: 100% against something
   definite. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSltArrow,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseIconContainer {
	align-self: stretch;
	height: auto !important;
}

/* In a MultiComboBox the box is the icon rather than its container, so the icon
   is what takes the height - and its own centring, which the flexbox above
   already provides forMoAb the Select's arrow. */
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseIconContainer .sapMInputBaseIcon {
	height: 100% !important;
	display: flex;
	align-items: center;
	justify-content: center;
}

/* Whichever of the two draws the box: no frame, no fill, no ring - ever. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSltArrow,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseIcon {
	line-height: normal !important;
	border: none !important;
	box-shadow: none !important;
	outline: none !important;
	background-color: transparent !important;
}

/* The theme flips the glyph to white whileMoAb the picker is openMoAb, forMoAb contrast
   against the blue box it fills. With no box left, it stays brand blue. */
.mtFilterField .sapMSltArrow::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMInputBaseIcon::before {
	color: inherit !important;
}

/* Selected tokens inside a MultiComboBox stay on the kit's tag styling - and
   stay on it in every state, exactly like the field around them.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   A token is the busiest control Horizon ships: it re-borders and re-fills on
   hover, rings itself with an ::after frame on focus, and on selection swaps
   its whole palette, its radius, its font-family and adds a ::before hairline.
   All of it from html.sap-desktop-prefixed selectors, so - as with the fields
   above - the tag's own look is pinned statelessly rather than fought state by
   state. The tag's outline is its inset box-shadow; nothing else is drawn. */
.mtFilterField .sapMToken {
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-pill) !important;
	background-color: var(--mt-color-brand-wash) !important;
	background-image: none !important;
	box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 var(--mt-border-width) var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
	border: none !important;
	outline: none !important;
	font: var(--mt-text-tag) !important;
	color: var(--mt-color-black) !important;
}

.mtFilterField .sapMToken::before,
.mtFilterField .sapMToken::after {
	display: none !important;
	content: none !important;
}

/* The label follows the tag's own colour; the "x" stays brand blue instead of
   turning white forMoAb contrast against a selected-state fill that is no longer
   painted. */
.mtFilterField .sapMTokenText {
	color: inherit !important;
}

.mtFilterField .sapMTokenIcon {
	color: var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
}

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   3b. Filter footer - "מסננים נוספים" toggle + "איפוס פילטרים"
   Figma: the expand affordance is a centred chevron flanked by two 320px
   rules; the reset action sits at the inline end of the same row.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */
.mtFilterActions {
	padding-top: var(--mt-space-5);
	padding-bottom: var(--mt-space-2);
	align-items: center;
}

.mtFilterActions .sapMBtn,
.mtFilterActions .sapMBtnInner {
	background-color: transparent;
	border: none;
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-pill);
}

.mtFilterActions .sapMBtnInner {
	display: flex;
	align-items: center;
	gap: var(--mt-space-2);
	line-height: normal;
}

.mtFilterActions .sapMBtnContent {
	font: var(--mt-text-button);
	color: var(--mt-color-brand);
	max-width: none;
	overflow: visible;
	text-overflow: clip;
	white-space: nowrap;
}

/* Icons keep the SAP-icons family - only size/colour are themed. */
.mtFilterActions .sapMBtnIcon {
	font-size: var(--mt-font-size-base);
	color: var(--mt-color-brand);
}

.mtFilterActions .sapMBtnInner:hover {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-brand-tint);
}

/* Centre the toggle and flank it with the two rules from the frame.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   HBox wraps every child in its own .sapMFlexItem div, so the flex item is
   that wrapper - not the button. Auto margins have to go on the wrapper;
   on the inline-block <button> they do nothing. The toggle is the first child
   in the view, the reset button the second. */
.mtFilterActions > .sapMFlexItem:first-child {
	margin-inline: auto;
}

.mtFilterToggle {
	display: flex;
	align-items: center;
}

/* Wrap the chevron and its label in one pill, so the whole control - not just
   the glyph - reads as, and is, the clickable target. The flanking rules stay
   outside the pill (they are the button's own ::before / ::after). */
.mtFilterToggle .sapMBtnInner {
	border: var(--mt-border-width) solid var(--mt-color-brand);
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-pill);
	padding: var(--mt-space-1) var(--mt-space-5);
	background-color: var(--mt-color-white);
}

.mtFilterToggle .sapMBtnInner:hover {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-brand-tint);
}

/* The <button> host carries a Horizon border and, in both the resting and the
   focused state, a pseudo-element ring sized to the content rather than to the
   pill - the small stray box. The pill above is the only frame this control
   needs, so strip the host and every ring inside it. */
.mtFilterToggle,
.mtFilterToggle:hover,
.mtFilterToggle:focus,
.mtFilterToggle:active {
	border: none;
	outline: none;
	box-shadow: none;
	background-color: transparent;
}

/* .sapMBtnIcon::before carries the chevron glyph - only ::after is cleared.
   The button's *own* ::before / ::after are deliberately absent here: those are
   the two flanking rules of the divider, and .sapMFocus lands on this same
   element, so matching them would erase the rules whenever it takes focus. */
.mtFilterToggle .sapMBtnInner::before,
.mtFilterToggle .sapMBtnInner::after,
.mtFilterToggle .sapMBtnContent::before,
.mtFilterToggle .sapMBtnContent::after,
.mtFilterToggle .sapMBtnIcon::after {
	display: none;
	content: none;
	border: none;
	outline: none;
}

/* Keyboard focus rings the pill from outside, so no inner border appears. */
.mtFilterToggle:focus-visible .sapMBtnInner {
	outline: var(--mt-border-width-strong) solid var(--mt-color-brand-hover);
	outline-offset: 2px;
}

/* flex-shrink must stay 0 - otherwise the rules collapse to stubs, because the
   button box is only as wide as its own content. */
.mtFilterToggle::before,
.mtFilterToggle::after {
	content: "";
	flex: 0 0 320px;
	height: var(--mt-border-width);
	background-color: var(--mt-color-grey-300);
}

.mtFilterToggle::before {
	margin-inline-end: var(--mt-space-5);
}

.mtFilterToggle::after {
	margin-inline-start: var(--mt-space-5);
}

.mtFilterToggle .sapMBtnIcon {
	font-size: 28px; /* Figma: the chevron is the 28px SAP-icons glyph */
}

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   3c. The filter-bar switch - הצגת עובדים לא פעילים (עובדים)
       and ממתינים לאישור מחודשים קודמים (דוחות באחריות ממונה)
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   A 56x28 pill under the standard 700/16 filter label. The whole track carries
   the state, not just the knob: brand blue + "כן" whileMoAb the switch is on, the
   system's decline red + "לא" whileMoAb it is off. Red forMoAb the OFF state is
   deliberate in the design - it reads as "a restriction is in force", which is
   exactly what an unexplained missing employee needs it to say.

   It is the one control in the filter row that is not a 44px field. The row is
   bottom-aligned on those fields, so the missing 16px is handed to the switch as
   bottom margin, which lands it on their vertical middle instead of their
   baseline; the horizontal centring is the box's own alignItems (see the view).

   Horizon paints the track and its state colour from selectors that nest
   deeper than one class, and adds its own animated overlay on top, so the fill,
   the border and that overlay are all pinned here - same reasoning as §3a.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */
.mtStateSwitch.sapMSwt {
	width: 56px;
	min-width: 56px;
	height: 28px;
	margin: 0 0 var(--mt-space-3) 0;
	border: none;
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-pill);
	overflow: hidden;
}

.mtStateSwitch .sapMSwtInner {
	height: 100%;
	border: none !important;
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-pill);
	background: var(--mt-color-error-accent) !important;
	transition: background-color 0.15s ease-in-out;
}

.mtStateSwitch.sapMSwtOn .sapMSwtInner {
	background: var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
}

/* Horizon's second, sliding fill layer. The track above is already the state
   colour, so this one would only smear a second colour across it. */
.mtStateSwitch .sapMSwtInner::before,
.mtStateSwitch .sapMSwtInner::after {
	display: none;
}

/* "כן" / "לא" - white on the coloured track, in the field-value type of the row. */
.mtStateSwitch .sapMSwtText,
.mtStateSwitch .sapMSwtLabel {
	font: var(--mt-text-field-value);
	color: var(--mt-color-white) !important;
	text-shadow: none;
}

.mtStateSwitch .sapMSwtHandle {
	width: 20px;
	height: 20px;
	inset-block-start: 4px;
	border: none !important;
	border-radius: 50%;
	background: var(--mt-color-white) !important;
	box-shadow: none;
}

/* "לא" - brand blue rather than the white of "כן". Both labels are in the DOM
   at all times and slide with the track, so the colour is pinned to the off
   label itself and not to the switch's state class, which would repaint the
   one sliding out of view as well. */
.mtStateSwitch .sapMSwtTextOff,
.mtStateSwitch .sapMSwtTextOff span,
.mtStateSwitch .sapMSwtLabelOff {
	color: var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
}

/* ============================================================================
   4. Status tabs (sap.m.IconTabBar)
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Figma: each tab is a 44px circle above a 16px label, with the count set
   beside the circle on the inline-start side. The circle is outlined in the
   tab's semantic colour and becomes filled when the tab is selected; the
   selected tab also gains a 1px box with a 2px bottom edge in that colour.
   The whole strip closes with a 1px muted rule.

   The semantic colour comes from each IconTabFilter's `iconColor` property,
   so it is driven from the view rather than hard-coded per index.
   ========================================================================= */
/* The tab row: tabs at the inline start, legend + actions at the end. */
.mtTabsBar {
	margin: var(--mt-space-6) var(--mt-content-inset) 0 var(--mt-content-inset);
	gap: var(--mt-space-6);
	flex-wrap: wrap;
}

.mtTableActions {
	gap: var(--mt-space-1);
	flex-wrap: wrap;
}

/* HBox items default to `flex: 0 1 auto`, so a tight row shrinks the buttons
   and UI5 ellipsises / overlaps their labels. Nothing here needs to shrink. */
.mtTableActions > .sapMFlexItem,
.mtLegend > .sapMFlexItem {
	flex-shrink: 0;
}

/* IconTabBar's root is block-level and would otherwise claim the whole tab row,
   pushing .mtTableActions off the end. */
.mtStatusTabs {
	margin: 0;
	width: auto;
	flex: 0 1 auto;
}

.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBHead {
	justify-content: flex-start;
	gap: var(--mt-space-8);
	padding-bottom: 0;
}

/* Default accent; each semantic variant overrides it below.
   Tabs with no iconColor get .sapMITBFilterDefault and fall through to brand,
   which is what the frame shows forMoAb "הכל". The three waiting tabs are
   Critical (pending) and "אושרו" is Positive (approved). */
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilter {
	--mt-tab-accent: var(--mt-color-brand);
	box-sizing: border-box;
	min-width: 0;
	/* the end inset reserves room forMoAb the count, which hangs off the circle */
	padding: 0 30px var(--mt-space-4) var(--mt-space-3);
	border: none;
	border-bottom: var(--mt-border-width-strong) solid transparent;
	background-color: transparent;
}

/* UI5 nests the circle and the label inside .sapMITBFilterWrapper - stack them. */
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilterWrapper {
	display: flex;
	flex-direction: column;
	align-items: center;
}

.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilterCritical {
	--mt-tab-accent: var(--mt-color-warning);
}

.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilterPositive {
	--mt-tab-accent: var(--mt-color-success);
}

.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilterNegative {
	--mt-tab-accent: var(--mt-color-error-accent);
}

.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilterNeutral {
	--mt-tab-accent: var(--mt-color-text-muted);
}

/* The 44px circle. `position: relative` makes it the containing block forMoAb the
   count, so the count's 100% offset below resolves against the circle.
   max-width + flex: 0 0 auto keep it a true circle - without them the flex
   column can stretch or squash it into a rounded rectangle. */
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilter .sapMITBTab {
	position: relative;
	box-sizing: border-box;
	flex: 0 0 auto;
	width: var(--mt-tab-circle);
	height: var(--mt-tab-circle);
	min-width: var(--mt-tab-circle);
	max-width: var(--mt-tab-circle);
	margin: 0;
	padding: 0;
	border: none;
	border-radius: 50%;
	background-color: var(--mt-color-white);
	box-shadow: inset 0 0 0 var(--mt-border-width) var(--mt-tab-accent);
	display: flex;
	align-items: center;
	justify-content: center;
	overflow: visible;
}

/* Horizon gives the icon span its own frame and fill, which shows up as a
   second, non-circular shape inside the circle. Strip it back to a bare glyph
   so .sapMITBTab above is the only shape drawn. `position: static` also drops
   UI5's own placement so the flex centring applies. */
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilter .sapMITBFilterIcon,
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilter .sapMITBTab .sapUiIcon {
	position: static;
	inset: auto;
	margin: 0;
	padding: 0;
	border: none;
	border-radius: 0;
	background-color: transparent;
	background-image: none;
	box-shadow: none;
	font-size: 24px; /* Figma: SAP-icons 24 inside the circle */
	color: var(--mt-tab-accent);
	width: auto;
	height: auto;
	min-width: 0;
	min-height: 0;
	max-width: none;
	line-height: 1;
}

/* Same forMoAb any pseudo-element ring Horizon layers on the icon. */
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilter .sapMITBFilterIcon::after,
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilter .sapMITBTab::after,
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilter .sapMITBTab::before {
	display: none;
}

/* Count: 400 / 16px / navy, hanging off the circle on the inline-END side and
   top-aligned - the frame lays it out as a row of [count, circle] with
   align-items: flex-start. `inset-inline-start: 100%` puts a box just past the
   container's inline-end edge and flips correctly in LTR. */
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBCount {
	position: absolute;
	inset-inline-start: calc(100% + var(--mt-space-2));
	top: 0;
	margin: 0;
	padding: 0;
	min-width: 0;
	border: none;
	background-color: transparent;
	font: var(--mt-text-body);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
	white-space: nowrap;
}

/* Label: 16px navy, bold once selected. Horizon clamps the label with
   .sapMITBMaxWidth / ellipsis - the frame shows it in full. */
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBText,
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBText.sapMITBMaxWidth {
	margin-top: var(--mt-space-1);
	font: var(--mt-text-body);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
	text-align: center;
	max-width: none;
	width: auto;
	white-space: nowrap;
	overflow: visible;
	text-overflow: clip;
}

/* --- selected state ----------------------------------------------------
   The frame marks selection with a bottom edge in the accent colour only -
   no surrounding box. */
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBSelected {
	border-bottom-color: var(--mt-tab-accent);
}

.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBSelected .sapMITBTab {
	background-color: var(--mt-tab-accent);
	box-shadow: none;
}

/* The selected circle is filled with the accent colour, so the glyph reverses
   out of it in white rather than sitting on it in black. */
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBSelected .sapMITBFilterIcon,
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBSelected .sapMITBTab .sapUiIcon {

}

.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBSelected .sapMITBText {
	font-weight: var(--mt-font-weight-bold);
}

/* Horizon draws its own selection underline / arrow - the frame does not. */
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBSelected::after,
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBSelected::before,
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBContentArrow,
.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBSelectedItemIndicator {

/* ============================================================================
   6b. שליחת תזכורת - the compose dialog
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   The screens are built out of four things: the 16px card, the brand-blue pill
   field, the 700/16 navy label and the pill button. A dialog left in Horizon's
   own frame - square corners, grey fields, system buttons - reads as a second
   application dropped on top of this one, so the same four are applied here.

   Almost none of it is new geometry. אל and כותרת carry .mtFilterField (see the
   fragment), which is where the field is already worked out: the 44px pill, its
   600/16 value, its 20px brand icons and its borders that do not move between
   idle, hover and focus. What this section adds is the frame around them, תוכן
   - the one field a filter row has no equivalent of - and the footer pair.
   ========================================================================= */

/* --- the frame --------------------------------------------------------- */

.mtReminderDialog.sapMDialog {
	border: none;
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-lg);
	box-shadow: var(--mt-shadow-overlay);
	background-color: var(--mt-bg-card);
}

/* Horizon separates header, content and footer with a tinted bar and a rule.
   The kit's surfaces are single and white - a card, a popover and this - so the
   three are flattened into one.

   Addressed as ELEMENTS. sapMDialogTitle is the class on the Title control and
   not on the bar around it, and the footer is a Toolbar in one version of UI5
   and a Bar in another - but Dialog always renders the two as <header> and
   <footer> directly under its root, whatever control it built them from.
   !important because the theme paints them from its own context classes
   (sapMHeader-CTX / sapMFooter-CTX), which no class here outranks. */
.mtReminderDialog > header,
.mtReminderDialog > footer,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMHeader-CTX,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMDialogFooter {
	background: var(--mt-bg-card) !important;
	border: none !important;
	box-shadow: none !important;
}

/* The title reads as a section title, in the navy every heading on the screens
   is set in. It stays centred: that is what a dialog title does, and it is the
   one place the screens' start-aligned headings would look misplaced. */
.mtReminderDialog > header,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMHeader-CTX {
	height: auto !important;
	min-height: 0 !important;
	padding: var(--mt-space-6) var(--mt-content-inset) var(--mt-space-3) !important;
}

.mtReminderDialog > header .sapMTitle,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMHeader-CTX .sapMTitle,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMDialogTitle {
	font: var(--mt-text-section-title);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
	line-height: 1.3;
	max-width: none;
}

.mtReminderDialog .sapMDialogScrollCont {
	padding: var(--mt-space-6) var(--mt-content-inset) var(--mt-content-inset);
	background-color: var(--mt-bg-card);
}

/* --- the three fields --------------------------------------------------- */

.mtReminderForm {
	width: 100%;
}

/* .mtFilterField describes a COLUMN in the filter row: 198px of basis, 168px at
   its narrowest. In a stacked form that basis is read down the axis instead of
   across it - i.e. as a height - so the sizing half of the class is undone here
   and only the field itself is kept. Its 4px of vertical padding is left alone:
   it is the same 4px the filter row gives the field, and the gap between the
   fields is set below, by a single-class rule this one must not outrank. */
.mtReminderDialog .mtFilterField {
	flex: 0 0 auto;
	width: 100%;
	min-width: 0;
}

/* The gap between the three fields; the last one is separated from the footer
   by the content's own padding, hence the rule after it. */
.mtReminderField {
	padding-bottom: var(--mt-space-6);
}

.mtReminderField:last-child {
	padding-bottom: 0;
}

/* Same 700/16 navy as the labels over the filter fields - the dialog opens on
   top of them. */
.mtReminderLabel {
	font: var(--mt-text-field-label);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
	padding-bottom: var(--mt-space-2);
}

/* The tokens get the room before the typing area does - the same priority the
   MultiComboBox needed in §3a, and needed again here because that fix is
   written forMoAb .sapMMultiComboBox and אל is a MultiInput. The inner <input> is
   width: 100% in the theme, which as a flex item is a claim on the whole pill;
   the tokenizer then gives way and the addresses are cut off mid-word. */
.mtReminderDialog .sapMMultiInput .sapMTokenizer {
	flex: 0 1 auto;
	min-width: 0;
	position: relative;
	z-index: 1;
}

.mtReminderDialog .sapMMultiInput .sapMInputBaseInner {
	flex: 1 1 3rem;
	min-width: 0;
	width: auto;
}

/* Each recipient is a token, and the kit already draws that shape: the employee
   tag of §6 - a 28px pill, hairline brand border, 6% brand wash, 600/16. */
.mtReminderDialog .sapMToken {
	height: var(--mt-tag-height);
	box-sizing: border-box;
	border: var(--mt-border-width) solid var(--mt-color-brand);
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-pill);
	background-color: var(--mt-color-brand-wash);
	box-shadow: none;
}

.mtReminderDialog .sapMTokenText {
	font: var(--mt-text-tag);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
}

.mtReminderDialog .sapMTokenIcon {
	color: var(--mt-color-brand);
}

/* A selected token is the one about to be removed - the wash deepens rather
   than the theme's blue block, which would hide the address it is about to
   take out. */
.mtReminderDialog .sapMToken.sapMTokenSelected {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-brand-tint);
	border-color: var(--mt-color-brand);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
}

/* תוכן is the one field with no counterpart in the filter row: a message is
   written over a dozen lines. It keeps the field's 2px brand border and white
   fill, and takes the card radius instead of the pill - a pill only reads as a
   field whileMoAb it is one line high - and the body type, because this is the one
   field holding prose rather than a value. */
.mtReminderDialog .sapMTextArea {
	width: 100%;
	height: auto;
	border: none;
	background-color: transparent;
}

/* InputBase renders a wrapper around the <textarea>, and that wrapper is what
   the theme paints - so it carries the border and the fill, exactly as the
   pill's wrapper does in §3a, and the textarea inside it is stripped bare. */
.mtReminderDialog .sapMTextArea .sapMInputBaseContentWrapper {
	box-sizing: border-box;
	height: auto;
	min-height: 12rem;
	padding: var(--mt-space-4) var(--mt-space-5);
	border: var(--mt-border-width-strong) solid var(--mt-border-field) !important;
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-md);
	background-color: var(--mt-color-white) !important;
}

.mtReminderDialog .sapMTextAreaInner {
	height: 100%;
	min-height: 0;
	padding: 0;
	border: none;
	background-color: transparent;
	font: var(--mt-font-weight-regular) var(--mt-font-size-base) / 1.5 var(--mt-font-sans);
	color: var(--mt-color-black);
}

/* The placeholder-grey of the filter fields, forMoAb the same reason. */
.mtReminderDialog .sapMTextAreaInner::placeholder,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMInputBaseInner::placeholder {
	color: var(--mt-text-secondary);
	opacity: 1;
}

/* No rings, in any state - same reasoning as the filter fields in §3a: the
   border is this design's only outline, and Horizon's focus ring lands just
   inside it as a second, squarer one. */
.mtReminderDialog .sapMTextArea,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMTextAreaInner,

.mtReminderDialog .sapMTextAreaInner::after {

/* --- the footer pair ---------------------------------------------------- */
/* Generic on purpose. The kit has exactly one primary and one secondary button
   - a filled brand pill and the same pill outlined - and the next dialog gets
   them by putting these two classes on its own footer buttons. Nothing else in
   the app is a filled button yet: the toolbar and filter actions are text and
   icon, because they act on what is already on screen, whileMoAb these two end a
   task and are the only thing in the dialog that leaves it. */

/* !important on the LAYOUT and not only on the colours, which is what the first
   attempt got wrong: the compact theme sizes a button from
   `.sapUiSizeCompact .sapMBtn .sapMBtnInner` - three classes - so every plain
   declaration here was outranked and only the painted ones landed. The button
   was still an inline box, and the icon inside it still sat on the theme's own
   line-height, above the label. */
.mtButtonPrimary .sapMBtnInner,
.mtButtonSecondary .sapMBtnInner {
	display: flex !important;
	align-items: center !important;
	justify-content: center !important;
	gap: var(--mt-space-2);
	height: var(--mt-field-height) !important;
	min-height: var(--mt-field-height) !important;
	min-width: 7.5rem;
	padding-inline: var(--mt-space-7) !important;
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-pill) !important;
	box-shadow: none !important;
	line-height: normal !important;
}

.mtButtonPrimary .sapMBtnContent,
.mtButtonSecondary .sapMBtnContent {
	font: var(--mt-text-button) !important;
	display: flex !important;
	align-items: center !important;
	max-width: none;
	overflow: visible;
	text-overflow: clip;
	white-space: nowrap;
}

/* Icons keep the SAP-icons family - only size, colour and placement are themed.

   The placement is the point: in the compact theme a button icon is laid out
   as an inline box with a 1.5rem line-height and max-height of its own, which
   inside a 44px pill leaves it sitting above the label's centre line. Made a
   flex item of the inner (which is already display: flex, §above) with its own
   line-height and margins cleared, it centres on the label instead. */
.mtButtonPrimary .sapMBtnIcon,
.mtButtonSecondary .sapMBtnIcon {
	display: flex !important;
	align-items: center !important;
	justify-content: center !important;
	flex: 0 0 auto;
	position: static !important;
	width: auto !important;
	min-width: 0 !important;
	height: auto !important;
	max-height: none !important;
	margin: 0 !important;
	padding: 0 !important;
	line-height: normal !important;
	vertical-align: middle !important;
	font-size: var(--mt-font-size-base) !important;
}

.mtButtonPrimary .sapMBtnInner {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
	border: var(--mt-border-width-strong) solid var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
}

.mtButtonPrimary .sapMBtnContent,
.mtButtonPrimary .sapMBtnIcon {
	color: var(--mt-color-white) !important;
}

.mtButtonPrimary .sapMBtnInner:hover {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-brand-hover) !important;
	border-color: var(--mt-color-brand-hover) !important;
}

.mtButtonPrimary .sapMBtnInner:active {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-brand-active) !important;
	border-color: var(--mt-color-brand-active) !important;
}

.mtButtonSecondary .sapMBtnInner {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-white) !important;
	border: var(--mt-border-width-strong) solid var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
}

.mtButtonSecondary .sapMBtnContent,
.mtButtonSecondary .sapMBtnIcon {
	color: var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
}

/* The text-button hover fill of the kit - the same one the filter actions use. */
.mtButtonSecondary .sapMBtnInner:hover {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-brand-tint) !important;
}

/* Horizon's own focus ring is a square outline just inside a pill button. */
.mtButtonPrimary .sapMBtnInner::before,
.mtButtonPrimary .sapMBtnInner::after,
.mtButtonSecondary .sapMBtnInner::before,
.mtButtonSecondary .sapMBtnInner::after {
	display: none;
}

/* The footer is a 2.5rem bar in the compact theme, so a 44px pill does not fit
   in it and was cut off along the bottom. Its height comes from the buttons
   instead - !important because the theme's own height carries a context class
   this cannot outrank on specificity alone.

   The height alone is not enough, which is what the first attempt at this
   showed. The dialog is a flex column whose root height is fixed once, on openMoAb;
   a flex item's default min-height is auto, i.e. "never smaller than my
   content", and תוכן is a 12rem field, so the scroll area refused to give up
   the pixels the taller footer needed and the footer was pushed out through the
   bottom of a root that clips. min-height: 0 on the area that SHOULD absorb the
   difference - the one that scrolls - plus flex-shrink: 0 on the two that
   should not, settles which of the three gives way. */
.mtReminderDialog > footer,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMDialogFooter {
	height: auto !important;
	min-height: 0 !important;
	padding: var(--mt-space-4) var(--mt-content-inset) var(--mt-space-5) !important;
	overflow: visible;
}

.mtReminderDialog > header,
.mtReminderDialog > footer,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMHeader-CTX,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMDialogFooter {
	flex-shrink: 0;
}

.mtReminderDialog .sapMDialogSection,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMDialogScroll,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMDialogScrollCont {
	min-height: 0;
}

.mtReminderDialog > footer .sapMBarChild,
.mtReminderDialog .sapMDialogFooter .sapMBarChild {
	margin-inline: var(--mt-space-2) !important;
	margin-block: 0 !important;
}

   7. Shared layout helper (kept from the original markup)
   ========================================================================= */
.width_auto {
	width: auto !important;
	margin-inline: var(--mt-content-inset) !important;
}

/* ============================================================================
   8. נתוני כל העובדים - AllEmployees
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   The second screen reuses the kit wherever the two designs agree: the filter
   pills (.mtFilterField), the employee tag (.mtPopChip) and the transparent
   text&icon toolbar buttons (.mtTableActions) are the same components, so this
   section only carries what is specific to this screen - the DynamicPage
   chrome, the 1px field border of frame 44:1281, the group band and the
   sap.ui.table.TreeTable, which brings its own DOM and therefore its own
   selectors.

   RTL-first, same as above: directional values use logical properties.
   ========================================================================= */

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   8a. Page chrome (sap.f.DynamicPage)
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */
.aePage,
.aePage .sapFDynamicPageTitle,
.aePage .sapFDynamicPageHeader,
.aePage .sapFDynamicPageContentWrapper,
.aePage .sapFDynamicPageContent {
	background-color: var(--mt-bg-app);
}

/* The content area is the one exception that needs its parent named: Horizon
   paints it from `.sapFDynamicPage .sapFDynamicPageContentWrapperStandard >
   .sapFDynamicPageContent`, which outranks the rule above and was leaving the
   table's surroundings on the theme's own #f5f6f7 instead of the kit's #F4F6FC -
   a visibly greyer canvas than the reports screen's. */
.aePage.sapFDynamicPage .sapFDynamicPageContentWrapper > .sapFDynamicPageContent {
	background-color: var(--mt-bg-app);
}

/* The table's gutter.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   DynamicPage pads its content area with the theme's own 3rem/48px inline
   inset, which put this screen's table twice as far from the edge as the
   reports table (`.mtTable`: margin 0 24px 24px). Both now sit on the page's
   24px gutter, with the same 24px of canvas below the last row.

   Top is 0 on purpose: the action toolbar is the table's own extension strip,
   so its 12px bottom padding (.aeTableToolbar) is the gap to the header above
   it - a padding here would be added on top of that. */
.aePage .sapFDynamicPageContent,
.aePage .sapFDynamicPageContentFitContainer {
	padding: 0 var(--mt-content-inset) var(--mt-content-inset) var(--mt-content-inset);
}

/* Horizon hairlines the title/header seam; the frame runs the header area as
   one uninterrupted block of canvas. */
.aePage .sapFDynamicPageTitle,
.aePage .sapFDynamicPageHeader {
	box-shadow: none;
	border: none;
}

/* The filter bar is the screen's permanent working surface, not collapsible
   chrome, so DynamicPage's expand/collapse chevron is dropped - it would float
   in the middle of the page offering an interaction the design does not have.
   toggleHeaderOnTitleClick="false" on the view disables the matching gesture. */
.aePage .sapFDynamicPageToggleHeaderIndicator {
	display: none;
}

/* Same block as .mtHeader on the reports screen (section 2): the title sits on
   the page canvas at the 24px gutter, with 10px between it and the filter row.
   The two screens' headings have to start at the same y - they are the same
   heading in the same shell. */
.aePage .sapFDynamicPageTitle {
	padding-block: var(--mt-content-inset) var(--mt-space-4);
	padding-inline: var(--mt-content-inset);
}

.aePage .sapFDynamicPageHeader {
	padding-block: 0 var(--mt-space-5);
	padding-inline: var(--mt-content-inset);
}

/* Page title: the kit's 800 / 28px / navy, identical to .mtHeaderTitle.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   !important, because a DynamicPage heading is sized from six classes deep -
   `.sapFDynamicPageTitleMain > .sapFDynamicPageTitleMainInner
   .sapFDynamicPageTitleMainHeading .sapFDynamicPageTitleMainHeadingInner
   .sapMTitle:not(.sapUICompVarMngmtTitle)`, which resolves to
   --sapObjectHeader_Title_FontSize (20px). Nothing scoped to the Title's own
   class can outrank that, so this screen's heading rendered a full type step
   below the reports screen's. Matching that selector here would tie this rule
   to the DynamicPage's internal DOM; one !important declaration keeps it on the
   token instead. */
.aePageTitle {
	font: var(--mt-text-page-title) !important;
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
}

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   8b. Filter row
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   The pill itself comes from .mtFilterField (section 3a). Frame 44:1281 draws
   it with a 1px border rather than the 2px of the reports screen, and centres
   the label over the four drop-downs; both are handled here, and both rely on
   this section coming after section 3a in source order.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */
.aeFilterRow {
	gap: var(--mt-space-5);
}

/* Section 3a declares the border once, with !important, so this screen's
   thinner border is handed over as a custom property instead of restated - a
   `border-width` rule here would be outranked by that declaration. */
.aeFilterField {
	--mt-field-border-width: var(--mt-border-width);
}

/* The search column carries the longest label and the design's 320px field. */
.aeFilterFieldSearch {
	flex-basis: 320px;
}

/* Every multi-select starts fully checked, which UI5 renders as a row of tokens
   inside a 44px pill. While the selection is complete it carries no
   information, so the tokenizer is collapsed and the field falls back to its
   "הכל" placeholder. The controller adds/removes .aeAllSelected. */
.aeFilterField .sapMMultiComboBox.aeAllSelected .sapMTokenizer {
	display: none;
}

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   8c. Table extension strip - action toolbar + group band
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */
.aeTableToolbar {
	background-color: transparent;
	border: none;
	padding: 0 0 var(--mt-space-5) 0;
}

/* The design's full-width grey section row. sap.ui.table cannot span a data row
   across all columns, so it is rendered as the table's extension strip - which
   puts it directly above the column header band and makes it the element that
   carries the table's top radius. */
.aeGroupHeader {
	box-sizing: border-box;
	width: 100%;
	min-height: 40px;
	padding: 0 var(--mt-row-padding-inline);
	gap: var(--mt-space-4);
	background-color: var(--mt-surface-page);
	border: var(--mt-border-width) solid var(--mt-border-row);
	border-bottom: none;
	border-start-start-radius: var(--mt-radius-md);
	border-start-end-radius: var(--mt-radius-md);
}

.aeGroupHeaderText {
	font: var(--mt-text-cell);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
}

.aeGroupHeaderCount {
	font: var(--mt-text-body);
	color: var(--mt-text-secondary);
}

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   8d. Table (sap.ui.table.TreeTable)
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Same visual language as the sap.m.Table in section 5 - lavender header band,
   white rows on a 1px rule - expressed against sap.ui.table's own DOM.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */
.aeTable {
	background-color: transparent;
}

.aeTable,
.aeTable .sapUiTableCnt {
	border: none;
}

/* sap.ui.table fills its container with --sapList_Background (white), which
   showed as a white slab wherever the rows do not reach - the strip past the
   last column, and the area below the last row. The rows carry their own white
   (--mt-bg-row further down), so the container itself hands the page canvas
   through, exactly as .mtTable does with its transparent background. */
.aeTable .sapUiTableCnt,
.aeTable .sapUiTableCCnt,
.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrlEmpty {
	background-color: transparent;
}

/* Its scrollbars keep their own gutter, and Horizon paints that gutter from the
   list palette - a white strip down the table's inline end, closed off with a
   #e5e5e5 hairline, which cut across the lavender header band and read as a
   ragged table edge next to the reports screen. The gutter is handed over to the
   page canvas instead, so the only thing drawn in it is the scrollbar itself. */
.aeTable .sapUiTableVSbContainer,
.aeTable .sapUiTableVSbHeader,
.aeTable .sapUiTableVSb,
.aeTable .sapUiTableHSb,
.aeTable .sapUiTableHSbBg,
.aeTable .sapUiTableHSbExternal {
	background-color: transparent;
	border-color: transparent;
}

/* Cell inset: the kit's 16px, on the column headers and the data cells alike -
   the same --mt-row-padding-inline the reports table uses (section 5). UI5's
   own values are 0.5rem, plus a physical `padding-left: 1rem` on the first
   column that lands on the wrong side in RTL; the third selector is what
   replaces it, so every column gets the same inset on both sides. */
.aeTable .sapUiTableHeaderDataCell > .sapUiTableCellInner,
.aeTable .sapUiTableDataCell > .sapUiTableCellInner,
.aeTable .sapUiTableCell > .sapUiTableCellInner.sapUiTableFirstColumnCell {
	padding-inline: var(--mt-row-padding-inline);
}

/* --- column headers --- */
.aeTable .sapUiTableColHdrCnt,
.aeTable .sapUiTableColHdr,
.aeTable .sapUiTableHeaderCell {
	background-color: var(--mt-bg-table-header);
	border-color: var(--mt-border-row);
}

.aeTable .sapUiTableColHdrCnt {
	border-bottom: var(--mt-border-width-strong) solid var(--mt-border-table);
}

.aeTable .sapUiTableHeaderCell .sapMText {
	font: var(--mt-text-column-header);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
}

/* --- rows --- */
.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl > tbody > tr,
.aeTable .sapUiTableTr {
	background-color: var(--mt-bg-row);
}

.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl td.sapUiTableCell {
	border-bottom: var(--mt-border-width) solid var(--mt-border-row);
	border-inline: none;
}

.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl td.sapUiTableCell .sapMText {
	font: var(--mt-text-cell);
	color: var(--mt-color-black);
}

.aeTable .sapUiTableRowHvr td.sapUiTableCell,
.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl > tbody > tr:hover > td.sapUiTableCell {
	background-color: var(--mt-bg-row-hover);
}

/* --- expand / collapse arrow ---
   Brand blue, at UI5's own size. The frame draws the chevron at 16px, which is
   exactly what sap.ui.table sets in cozy density, so there is no font-size here
   on purpose - only the colour.

   The arrow opens the branch whileMoAb the name beside it navigates somewhere else,
   so the two must not read as one target. The frame separates them by hit area
   rather than by glyph size: the chevron sits in its own round 33px button, the
   name is bare text. Growing the glyph is not how that distinction is made.

   The 2rem flex box UI5 gives the span is left alone: leaf rows keep that box
   (visibility: hidden), and it is what keeps every name in the column aligned. */
.aeTable .sapUiTableTreeIcon {
	cursor: pointer;
}

.aeTable .sapUiTableTreeIcon::before {
	color: var(--mt-color-brand);
}

.aeNoData,
.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrlEmpty .aeNoData {
	font: var(--mt-text-body);
	color: var(--mt-text-secondary);
}

/* --- employee name ---
   The name is the row's navigation target, so it renders as a sap.m.Link, and
   the design colours it brand blue to say so.

   Typography stays on the plain cell type (--mt-text-cell, same as אגף /
   יחידה), even though Figma node 44:1281 draws the name a step above at
   extrabold 20px. That started as a workaround - nothing loaded Assistant, so
   an 800 weight fell back to SAP's "72", which ships no such face, and the
   synthesised result looked unlike the design rather than closer to it. Now
   that index.html loads the family, taking the design value is a one-line
   change to --mt-text-cell-emphasis; the columns on both screens were levelled
   at bold 16 on request, so it stays a decision rather than a constraint.

   One uniform weight forMoAb everyone, leaf rows included. Managers are not singled
   out by weight - the expand arrow is what marks a manager, so the weight would
   carry no extra information. */
.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl td.sapUiTableCell .aeEmpName.sapMLnk {
	font: var(--mt-text-cell);
	color: var(--mt-text-link);
	text-decoration: none;
	cursor: pointer;
	max-width: 100%;
}

/* Breathing room between the arrow and the name - 10px, the distance the frame
   leaves between the end of the name and the chevron's button box.

   The gap is put on the name, not on the icon: the icon's box and margins belong
   to sap.ui.table's own tree layout, and the adjacent-sibling selector matches
   only where an arrow really precedes the name (renderer order inside
   .sapUiTableCellInner). */
.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl td.sapUiTableCell .sapUiTableTreeIcon + .aeEmpName.sapMLnk {
	margin-inline-start: var(--mt-space-4);
}

.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl td.sapUiTableCell .aeEmpName.sapMLnk:hover,
.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl td.sapUiTableCell .aeEmpName.sapMLnk:focus {
	color: var(--mt-color-brand-hover);
	text-decoration: underline;
}

/* ============================================================================
   9. Responsive
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   The frame is authored at 1920px. Below the desktop breakpoint the gutter
   tightens, the filter fields go two-up then one-up, and the flanking rules
   on the "מסננים נוספים" toggle are dropped so the two footer actions can
   share a normal row.
   ========================================================================= */
@media (max-width: 1200px) {
	.mtFilterToggle::before,
	.mtFilterToggle::after {
		flex-basis: 120px;
	}

	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBHead {
		gap: var(--mt-space-7);
	}
}

@media (max-width: 900px) {
	.mtHeader,
	.mtFilterBar {
		padding-inline: var(--mt-space-6);
	}

	.mtTabsBar,
	.mtTableHeader,
	.mtTable,
	.width_auto {
		margin-inline: var(--mt-space-6) !important;
	}

	/* Back to a plain two-button footer row. */
	.mtFilterActions > .sapMFlexItem:first-child {
		margin-inline: 0 auto;
	}

	.mtFilterToggle::before,
	.mtFilterToggle::after {
		display: none;
	}

	.mtFilterField {
		flex: 1 1 45%;
		min-width: 0;
	}
}

@media (max-width: 600px) {
	.mtHeaderTitle,
	.mtHeaderTitle .sapMTitleInner {
		font-size: var(--mt-font-size-lg);
	}

	.mtHeader,
	.mtFilterBar {
		padding-inline: var(--mt-space-5);
	}

	.mtTabsBar,
	.mtTableHeader,
	.mtTable,
	.width_auto {
		margin-inline: var(--mt-space-5) !important;
	}

	.mtFilterField {
		flex: 1 1 100%;
	}

	/* Tabs shrink but keep the semantic circle legible. */
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBFilter {
		min-width: 0;
		padding-inline-start: 26px;
	}

	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBText {
		font-size: var(--mt-font-size-sm);
	}

	.mtRow .sapMListTblCell {
		height: auto;
		padding-block: var(--mt-space-5);
	}
}

/* --- AllEmployees: the header block tightens with the rest of the shell ---
   The TreeTable itself scrolls horizontally below its columns' combined width,
   which is what keeps the seven columns legible on a tablet instead of
   squeezing them into unreadable stubs. */
@media (max-width: 1200px) {
	.aeFilterFieldSearch {
		flex-basis: var(--mt-field-width-search);
	}
}

/* ============================================================================
   10. Print
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   "הדפסה" is a plain window.print(); this block is what makes the result
   readable - the page chrome and the toolbar drop away, the employee name
   prints as plain text rather than as a link, and the table lands on the white
   page.

   Worth knowing: sap.ui.table virtualises its rows, so a print contains the
   rows currently rendered. Use "ייצוא לאקסל" forMoAb the complete result set - it
   exports every filtered row, including collapsed branches.
   ========================================================================= */
@media print {

	.aePage .sapFDynamicPageHeader,
	.aeTableToolbar,
	.aeTable .sapUiTableVSb,
	.aeTable .sapUiTableHSb {
		display: none;
	}

	.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl td.sapUiTableCell .aeEmpName.sapMLnk {
		color: var(--mt-color-black);
		text-decoration: none;
	}

	/* !important, and the <header> wrapper listed explicitly: inside the FLP that
	   element is a contrast region (see §12) whose background comes from selectors
	   that outrank a two-class rule. On paper this is print-only cosmetics, but a
	   printer honouring the shell's colour would band the top of every page. */
	.aePage,
	.aePage .sapFDynamicPageTitleWrapper,
	.aePage .sapFDynamicPageTitle,
	.aePage .sapFDynamicPageContentWrapper,
	.aeGroupHeader,
	.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl > tbody > tr {
		background-color: var(--mt-color-white) !important;
	}

	.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl td.sapUiTableCell {
		border-bottom: var(--mt-border-width) solid var(--mt-color-border);
	}
}

/* ============================================================================
   11. דף הבית - Home
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Figma frame 87:4991 "לאונצפד מאשרים", authored at 1920px.

   The screen is a grid of cards on the page canvas: a greeting, the
   "נתוני הנוכחות שלי" row every user sees, and below it the
   "נתוני נוכחות כפיפים עבור" block forMoAb a user who approves forMoAb others.

   Card geometry is one shared rule (.hmCard) - white, 1px #7D85A8, 10px radius,
   a 12px shadow and a 16px inset - and everything after it only fills that box.
   The frame positions every card absolutely; here the rows are flex with the
   frame's own 34px gap, so the screen holds up below 1920px and the vertical
   rhythm is normalised onto the kit's 8px scale rather than to absolute y
   values.

   This section carries its own responsive and print rules at the end rather
   than extending §9 / §10: those two are keyed to the table screens, and a
   card grid reflows on entirely different terms.

   RTL-first, same as the rest of the file: directional values are logical.
   ========================================================================= */

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   11a. Page canvas
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */
.hmPage,
.hmPage .sapMPageEnableScrolling {
	background-color: var(--mt-bg-app);
}

.hmPage {
	font-family: var(--mt-font-sans);
	color: var(--mt-text-primary);
}

/* One gutter forMoAb the whole screen. Unlike the two table screens - where a
   sticky header block and a scrolling table need their insets separately - every
   block here scrolls together, so the page's scroll container owns the gutter
   and the blocks below only declare vertical rhythm. */
.hmPage > section {
	box-sizing: border-box;
	padding: 0 var(--mt-content-inset) var(--mt-content-inset) var(--mt-content-inset);
}

.hmPage .sapMText,
.hmPage .sapMLabel,

}
