}

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   11c. The card shell
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */
.hmCardRow {
	gap: var(--mt-card-gap);
}

.hmCard {
	box-sizing: border-box;
	flex: 0 1 auto;
	min-width: var(--mt-card-min-width);
	padding: var(--mt-space-6);
	gap: var(--mt-space-6);
	background-color: var(--mt-bg-card);
	border: var(--mt-border-width) solid var(--mt-color-text-muted);
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-md);
	box-shadow: var(--mt-shadow-card-raised);
	overflow: hidden;
}

/* סטטוס הדו"ח and הודעות are the frame's two 463px cards. */
.hmCardWide {
	flex-basis: var(--mt-card-width-wide);
	min-width: var(--mt-card-width-wide);
}

/* The frame draws all three of these at exactly 283px, and a fixed height is
   what it takes to get that - min-height alone did not.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   הודעות is the one card in the row whose content can outgrow the box: with
   only a minimum it stretched to its message list (317px forMoAb the two seeded
   messages), alignItems="Stretch" handed that height to סטטוס הדו"ח as well, and
   הזנת נוכחות - a Button, whose height the theme sets explicitly, so it does not
   stretch - stayed behind at its 283px minimum. The row came out 34px too tall
   with one card's bottom edge above the other two.

   The height also makes .hmMessagesCard's own overflow-y: auto do something.
   Nothing else in the row comes close to 283px, so nothing else is clipped. */
.hmMyCardRow .hmCard {
	height: var(--mt-card-height);
}

.hmSubCardRow .hmCard {
	min-height: var(--mt-card-min-height);
}

/* The bound card list and the static "צפייה בכל העובדים" launcher have to sit
   on one line, so the binding container is taken out of the box tree and its
   cards become flex items of .hmSubCardRow directly. Without this the whole
   bound list would be a single flex item and the launcher would be pushed onto
   a line of its own. */
.hmCardGroup {
	display: contents;
}

/* display: block on the title itself - sap.m.Title and sap.m.Link both render
   inline-block, and an inline-block in a column flexbox is laid out on a line
   box, which adds the font's descender space under a 26px title and pushed
   everything below it ~5px down the card. */
.hmCardTitle,
.hmCardTitle .sapMTitleInner {
	font: var(--mt-text-card-title);
	color: var(--mt-color-navy-deep);
}

.hmCardTitle {
	display: block;
}

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   11d. Launcher card (הזנת נוכחות / צפייה בכל העובדים)
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   The frame makes the whole card the click target, so it is a real sap.m.Button
   rather than a styled box - keyboard, focus and ARIA come with the control.
   With iconFirst="false" the button's inner span holds [title, glyph], and the
   column layout below turns that into the frame's "title at the top-start,
   64px glyph at the bottom-start".
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */

/* .hmCard's inset moves to the inner span - that is the box which fills the
   button - and the host keeps the frame. */
.hmLauncherCard {
	padding: 0;
	margin: 0;
	cursor: pointer;
}

/* Stateless, forMoAb the same reason as the filter pills in §3a: Horizon re-borders
   and re-fills a button on hover, focus and active from selectors that nest
   deeper than a single class. The card looks the same in all of them; hover is
   signalled by the border colour alone (below). */
.hmLauncherCard,
.hmLauncherCard:hover,
.hmLauncherCard:focus,
.hmLauncherCard:active {
	background-color: var(--mt-bg-card);
	border: var(--mt-border-width) solid var(--mt-color-text-muted);
	box-shadow: var(--mt-shadow-card-raised);
	outline: none;
}

.hmLauncherCard:hover {
	border-color: var(--mt-color-brand);
}

.hmLauncherCard:focus-visible {
	outline: var(--mt-border-width-strong) solid var(--mt-color-brand-hover);
	outline-offset: 2px;
}

/* The frame's stacking - title at the top, 64px glyph at the bottom - is set with
   explicit `order` further down rather than by relying on the markup: which of
   the icon and the content sap.m.Button renders first, and whether `iconFirst`
   reorders the DOM or only adds a class, is internal to the control and differs
   between themes. Ordering the two flex items by name is immune to all of it.
   space-between then pins each to its own end of the card. */
.hmLauncherCard .sapMBtnInner,
.hmLauncherCard .sapMBtnInner:hover,
.hmLauncherCard .sapMBtnInner:active {
	box-sizing: border-box;
	display: flex;
	flex-direction: column;
	justify-content: space-between;
	align-items: stretch;
	width: 100%;
	height: 100%;

	/* !important, and only here: an icon-and-text button is padded asymmetrically
	   by the theme to leave room forMoAb the glyph it expects beside the label -
	   `.sapUiSizeCompact .sapMBtnInner.sapMBtnText.sapMBtnIconFirst
	   {padding-right: 1.875rem}` - which is four classes and outranks anything
	   scoped to .hmLauncherCard. The card was coming out with a 30px inset on the
	   start side and 7px on the end, so its title sat off the 16px grid every
	   other card in the row is on. The glyph is not beside the label here (the
	   column layout below puts it at the bottom of the card), so there is nothing
	   forMoAb that reserved space to hold. */
	padding: var(--mt-space-6) !important;
	border: none;
	border-radius: 0;
	background-color: transparent;
	background-image: none;
	box-shadow: none;
	line-height: normal;
}

/* Horizon rings a focused / pressed button with a pseudo-element sized to the
   content rather than to the card - a stray box in the middle of it. */
.hmLauncherCard .sapMBtnInner::before,
.hmLauncherCard .sapMBtnInner::after,
.hmLauncherCard .sapMBtnContent::before,
.hmLauncherCard .sapMBtnContent::after {
	display: none;
	content: none;
	border: none;
	outline: none;
}

/* The title wraps - a card is 342px wide and the labels are Hebrew, so
   Horizon's nowrap + ellipsis would clip them. */
.hmLauncherCard .sapMBtnContent {
	order: 1;
	font: var(--mt-text-card-title);
	color: var(--mt-color-navy-deep);
	text-align: start;
	max-width: none;
	overflow: visible;
	text-overflow: clip;
	white-space: normal;
	line-height: normal;
}

/* The glyph, at the bottom inline end of the card.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Two theme behaviours have to be undone first, and neither is obvious:

   `position: static` - the theme positions a button's custom icon absolutely,
   which takes it out of the flex flow entirely. While it was out, `order` and
   `align-self` below did nothing and the glyph stayed pinned at the top inline
   end of the card, half of it clipped by the card's own overflow. `inset: auto`
   clears the offsets that came with it. This is the same undo as the filter
   field's chevron in §3a.

   The box, sized to the glyph - the glyph is 64px but the theme keeps the span it
   is drawn in at the compact density's 24px, and `height` alone could not lift it
   because the rule that holds it there is a *maximum*:
   `.sapUiSizeCompact .sapMBtnIcon {max-height: 1.5rem}`. A max-height clamps a
   larger height no matter how the two rules rank, so the 64px declaration below
   was being honoured and then thrown away, and the glyph - still drawn at 64px -
   overflowed a 24px box pinned to the bottom of the card until the card's own
   overflow: hidden cut it off at the border. Releasing the maximum is what makes
   the height mean anything; the flex centring then holds the glyph in the box
   whatever height its own outline is drawn at, which is the same treatment §3a
   gives a MultiComboBox's icon.

   align-self on the cross axis of a column flexbox: flex-start is the inline
   start - the right in RTL, directly under the card's title, which is where the
   frame puts both launcher glyphs - and it flips correctly in LTR. */
.hmPage .hmLauncherCard .sapMBtnIcon {
	box-sizing: border-box;
	position: static;
	inset: auto;
	order: 2;
	align-self: flex-start;
	flex: 0 0 auto;
	display: flex;
	align-items: center;

	/* flex-end, and this is the line that keeps the glyph inside the card.
	   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   The glyph is not drawn inside the box the font claims forMoAb it. The ::before
	   that carries it measures 16px wide at font-size 64 (an 0.25em advance) whileMoAb
	   its outline inks 53px, and RTL mirroring - `.sapUiIconMirrorInRTL::before
	   {transform: scale(-1,1)}`, which is what puts the clock and the plus on the
	   sides the frame draws them - sends all 53px of that off the *inline-start*
	   side of the 16px box. Both launcher glyphs behave the same way.

	   So it is the 16px box that has to be placed, and where it goes decides
	   whether the ink lands on the card or over its edge: parked at the inline
	   start the ink runs straight off the card, centred it overhangs by 15px - and
	   since align-self above puts the span's inline start flush on the card's 16px
	   inset, that overhang fell in the padding and was cut off by the border, which
	   read as a broken glyph. Parked at the inline end, the ink sweeps back
	   *across* the span rather than out of it, which leaves it on the card with its
	   leading edge on the frame's inset - to within the few px the two glyphs'
	   outlines differ by, which is as close as an icon font gets. */
	justify-content: flex-end;
	margin: 0;
	padding: 0 !important;
	/* Height is the 64px the frame draws; width is a *minimum* of it, because a
	   SAP icon is not square. create-entry-time is drawn wider than its em box, so
	   at font-size 64 its outline ran past a 64px span - and this span's inline
	   start is flush against the card's 16px inset, so the overflow crossed the
	   padding and was cut by the border, which read as a broken glyph. Sizing the
	   box to the glyph instead of the glyph to the box holds any icon inside the
	   card, whatever its aspect ratio; the anchor stays the inline start either
	   way, which is the edge the frame lines the glyph up on. */
	width: auto !important;
	height: var(--mt-launcher-icon) !important;
	min-width: var(--mt-launcher-icon);
	max-width: none;
	max-height: none !important;
	font-size: var(--mt-launcher-icon);
	line-height: 1 !important;
	color: var(--mt-color-brand);
}

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   11e. סטטוס הדו"ח
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Three counters on one line, each an 800/28 number with its 32px glyph on the
   inline-start side and a 400/16 label beneath. They are peers: the "כל השגיאות"
   total that used to lead the row in the decline red, ruled off from the rest,
   is gone, and with it the data-tone="error" cell this section styled.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */

/* Two children - title and counter row - pushed to the top and bottom of the

.hmStatusCard {
	justify-content: space-between;
}

.hmKpiRow {

/* Same technique as the wizard dots in §6: the "record" glyph draws a circle

/* A Link stretched by the card's align-items would underline across the whole

}

.hmMetricLabel,
.hmPage .hmMetricLabel.sapMText {
	font: var(--mt-text-body);
	color: var(--mt-color-navy-deep);
	min-width: 0;
	white-space: nowrap;
	overflow: hidden;
	text-overflow: ellipsis;
}

.hmMetricCount,
.hmPage .hmMetricCount.sapMText {
	font: var(--mt-text-cell);
	color: var(--mt-color-navy-deep);
	flex-shrink: 0;
}

/* Track and fill. The fill's width is a percentage set on the control (its
   share of the card), so only the colour is CSS - and it keys off data-tone,
   because a background colour cannot be bound the way an Icon's can. In RTL the
   fill starts at the right, which is what the frame draws. */
.hmMetricTrack {
	width: 100%;
	height: var(--mt-bar-height);
	background-color: var(--mt-color-border-quiet);
}

.hmMetricFill {
	height: var(--mt-bar-height);
	min-width: 0;
}

.hmMetricFill[data-tone="brand"] {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-brand);
}

.hmMetricFill[data-tone="warning"] {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-warning);
}

.hmMetricFill[data-tone="muted"] {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-text-muted);
}

.hmMetricFill[data-tone="error"] {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-error-accent);
}

.hmMetricFill[data-tone="success"] {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-success);
}

/* "עודכן מלפני 5 דקות" + the refresh glyph, at the card's bottom inline start.
   margin-top: auto is what pins it there however many stages the card lists -
   נוכחות חיילות has four, the rest three. */
.hmCardFooter {
	align-self: flex-start;
	margin-top: auto;

	/* The theme gives every button a 3px block margin. On a line pinned to the
	   bottom of the card that reads as 3px of extra card, not as spacing. */
	margin-block: 0;
	background-color: transparent;
	border: none;
	box-shadow: none;
}

.hmCardFooter .sapMBtnInner,
.hmCardFooter .sapMBtnInner:active {
	display: flex;
	align-items: center;
	gap: var(--mt-space-2);

	/* !important forMoAb the same reason as the launcher card's inner span: the
	   theme reserves ~30px on the start side of an icon-and-text button, which
	   held the refresh glyph 30px off the card's 16px content edge. */
	padding: var(--mt-space-1) var(--mt-space-2) !important;
	border: none;
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-pill);
	background-color: transparent;
	background-image: none;
	box-shadow: none;
	line-height: normal;
}

.hmCardFooter .sapMBtnInner:hover {
	background-color: var(--mt-color-brand-tint);
}

.hmCardFooter .sapMBtnContent {
	font: var(--mt-text-field-value);
	color: var(--mt-color-brand);
	max-width: none;
	overflow: visible;
	text-overflow: clip;
	white-space: nowrap;
}

/* The same undo as the launcher glyph in §11d, and required by the padding above:
   the theme positions a button's custom icon absolutely and reserves the ~30px of
   inline-start padding it is placed in. With that padding pinned back to the 6px
   this line actually wants, an absolutely-placed glyph is left sitting on top of
   "עודכן מלפני..." - so it has to rejoin the flow, where the inner span's `gap`
   spaces it off the label. */
.hmCardFooter .sapMBtnIcon {
	position: static;
	inset: auto;
	flex: 0 0 auto;
	margin: 0;
	color: var(--mt-color-brand);
	font-size: var(--mt-font-size-base);
}

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   11i. Responsive
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   The frame is authored at 1920px, where all five subordinate cards share one
   line. Below that the rows wrap on the cards' own 342px / 463px minimums; the
   only thing the breakpoints do is tighten the gutter and, once a 342px card no
   longer fits beside anything, let the cards go full width.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */
@media (max-width: 900px) {
	.hmPage > section {
		padding-inline: var(--mt-space-6);
		padding-bottom: var(--mt-space-6);
	}

	.hmCardRow {
		gap: var(--mt-space-7);
	}
}

@media (max-width: 600px) {
	.hmPage > section {
		padding-inline: var(--mt-space-5);
		padding-bottom: var(--mt-space-5);
	}

	.hmGreeting,
	.hmGreeting .sapMTitleInner {
		font-size: var(--mt-font-size-lg);
	}

	/* min-width has to go too, or a 342px card overflows a narrower viewport. */
	.hmCard,
	.hmCardWide,
	.hmMetricCard {
		flex-basis: 100%;
		min-width: 0;
		width: 100%;
	}

	/* Fixed heights are dropped: with one card per line there is no neighbour to
	   match, and a 283px box around two stages is mostly empty space. הודעות
	   אישיות keeps a maximum of its own below, so it still scrolls rather than
	   running the page long. */
	.hmMyCardRow .hmCard {
		height: auto;
		min-height: 0;
	}

	.hmMessagesCard {
		max-height: var(--mt-card-height);
	}

	.hmSubHeader {
		align-items: flex-start;
	}
}

/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   11j. Print
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   The print glyph, the month stepper and the אוכלוסיה field are controls, not
   content - a printed copy keeps the month it was taken in, in the label above
   the cards. Shadows go so the cards print as ruled boxes.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------ */
@media print {

	.hmPrintButton,
	.hmMonthArrow,
	.hmCardFooter {
		display: none;
	}

	.hmPage,
	.hmPage .sapMPageEnableScrolling {
		background-color: var(--mt-color-white);
	}

	.hmCard,
	.hmLauncherCard {
		box-shadow: none;
		break-inside: avoid;
		page-break-inside: avoid;
	}

	.hmMessagesCard {
		overflow: visible;
		max-height: none;
	}

	.hmPage .hmCardTitleLink.sapMLnk {
		color: var(--mt-color-navy-deep);
		text-decoration: none;
	}
}

/* ============================================================================
   12. Launchpad overrides
   ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Everything in this section is one screen looking different inside the Fiori
   launchpad than it does standalone, and there are exactly two reasons forMoAb it.

   (1) The launchpad's stylesheet is the later one. Standalone, the app's CSS is
       loaded after the theme's, so a rule of ours at the *same* specificity as
       the theme's wins on source order alone - and much of the file leans on
       that: `.mtTableActions .sapMBtnIcon` is two classes, and so is sap.m's own
       `.sapMBtn .sapMBtnIcon`. In the launchpad that order is reversed and every
       one of those ties flips to the theme. This is what turned the toolbar
       glyphs and the tab counts into the shell's own colours. The fix is not
       more specificity - it is `!important`, which takes source order out of it.

   (2) Two controls render a *contrast region* of their own, with the class
       written by the renderer rather than by the app:

     sap.f.DynamicPageRenderer      <header class="sapContrastPlus
                                             sapFDynamicPageTitleWrapper">
     sap.m.IconTabHeaderRenderer    <div class="sapMITH sapContrastPlus …">

   A contrast region is meant to sit on the shell's own colour, so a theme
   repaints everything inside it from its contrast palette - surfaces take the
   shell background, and content (icons, labels) flips to white to stay legible
   on it. Standalone under sap_horizon that palette is close enough to the
   canvas that nothing showed. Inside the launchpad it is the FLP's shell
   colour, and it arrived on both screens at once:

     נתוני כל העובדים   the <header> wrapper - which §8a never names, because it
                        paints the title and the header *inside* it - came up in
                        the FLP's colour behind them.

     דוחות באחריות ממונה  the status tabs' glyphs turned white, so an unselected
                        tab was a white icon on the white circle of §4 and
                        vanished; only the selected tab, whose circle is filled
                        with its accent colour, still showed its icon. The
                        design is the other way round: white circle, icon in the
                        tab's own colour.

   Both are fixed the same way - the design's own values are restated forMoAb the
   region, with !important, since the theme's contrast rules nest deeper than
   anything scoped to one of our classes. The regions themselves are left in
   place: the class is hard-coded in the renderers, so stripping it in JS would
   have to be redone on every re-render (the IconTabHeader re-renders on every
   tab click) and would fight the framework instead of the palette.

   `@media screen` on purpose: §10's print rules put this same chrome on white,
   and an !important declaration here would outrank them.
   ========================================================================= */
@media screen {

	/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   12a. נתוני כל העובדים - the DynamicPage's <header> wrapper
	   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   The wrapper holds the title plus the sticky placeholder that the filter
	   header moves into once it sticks, so the title and the header are restated
	   here too - whichever of them is inside the region at the time, it stays on
	   the page canvas. The tag is matched by class, not as `header`: the element's
	   tag follows sap.f.DynamicPageAccessibleLandmarkInfo and is only `<header>`
	   by default. */
	.aePage.sapFDynamicPage > .sapFDynamicPageTitleWrapper,
	.aePage.sapFDynamicPage > .sapFDynamicPageTitleWrapper .sapFDynamicPageTitle,
	.aePage.sapFDynamicPage > .sapFDynamicPageTitleWrapper .sapFDynamicPageHeader {
		background-color: var(--mt-bg-app) !important;
		background-image: none !important;
		box-shadow: none !important;
	}

	/* The same palette turns the text in that region white - which was invisible
	   the moment the background above went back to the light canvas, so it belongs
	   with the fix rather than after it. The heading is the region's only content
	   until the filter header sticks into it; .sapMLabel covers the filter labels
	   forMoAb when it does. */
	.aePage.sapFDynamicPage > .sapFDynamicPageTitleWrapper .sapMTitle,
	.aePage.sapFDynamicPage > .sapFDynamicPageTitleWrapper .sapMTitle .sapMTitleInner,
	.aePage.sapFDynamicPage > .sapFDynamicPageTitleWrapper .sapMLabel {
		color: var(--mt-text-primary) !important;
	}

	/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   12b. דוחות באחריות ממונה - status tabs
	   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   §4's own values, restated forMoAb the region. --mt-tab-accent still comes from
	   the IconTabFilter (§4 sets it there, per iconColor), so each tab keeps its
	   own colour - nothing is hard-coded per tab here either.

	   The selected block has to stay *after* the unselected one: .sapMITBSelected
	   sits on the same element as .sapMITBFilter, so both match a selected tab at
	   the same specificity and source order is what separates them. */
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBFilter .sapMITBTab {
		background-color: var(--mt-color-white) !important;
	}

	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBFilter .sapMITBFilterIcon,
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBFilter .sapMITBTab .sapUiIcon {
		color: var(--mt-tab-accent) !important;
	}

	/* The glyph is drawn by ::before, and the contrast palette colours it there as
	   well as on the span - the same undo as the filter field's chevron in §3a. */
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBFilter .sapMITBFilterIcon::before,
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBFilter .sapMITBTab .sapUiIcon::before {
		color: inherit !important;
	}

	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBSelected .sapMITBTab {
		background-color: var(--mt-tab-accent) !important;
	}

	/* Here white *is* the design: the selected circle is filled with the accent
	   colour, so its glyph reverses out of it. */
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBSelected .sapMITBFilterIcon,
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBSelected .sapMITBTab .sapUiIcon {
		color: var(--mt-color-white) !important;
	}

	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBSelected .sapMITBFilterIcon::before,
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBSelected .sapMITBTab .sapUiIcon::before {
		color: inherit !important;
	}

	/* Label and count sit on the page canvas, outside the circle - white there
	   would erase them, forMoAb the same reason as the heading in 12a. */
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBText,
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBText.sapMITBMaxWidth,
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBCount {
		color: var(--mt-text-primary) !important;
	}

	/* The strip itself keeps the page canvas: the region's background would
	   otherwise show as a band behind the tabs, the same way it did behind the
	   title in 12a.

	   Backgrounds only - deliberately no `box-shadow: none` on .sapMITBFilter:
	   that is the element that takes keyboard focus, and some themes draw the
	   focus ring on it as a shadow rather than an outline. */
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH,
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBHead,
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH .sapMITBFilter {
		background-color: transparent !important;
		background-image: none !important;
	}

	/* The rule that closes the strip.
	   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   §4's design note ("the whole strip closes with a 1px muted rule") was never
	   implemented here, because sap.m draws a line of its own: `.sapMITH` carries
	   `box-shadow: @_sap_m_IconTabBar_HeaderShadow`, and under sap_horizon that
	   parameter happened to land close enough to the design to pass. It is a theme
	   parameter, so in the launchpad it resolves to that theme's value instead -
	   the heavy dark bar under the tabs. Drawing the rule ourselves puts it on the
	   kit's own colour and takes the theme out of it.

	   An inset shadow rather than a border-bottom on purpose: it paints inside the
	   existing box, so the tabs keep their exact height and the selected tab's 2px
	   accent edge (§4) stays where it was instead of being pushed up a pixel.

	   `border-bottom: none` beside it because a theme is free to draw the same line
	   as a border rather than as a shadow, and .sapMITBContainerContent because
	   with expandable="false" that empty content box sits directly below the
	   header, where a border-top of its own would read as a second rule. */
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITH {
		border-bottom: none !important;
		box-shadow: inset 0 -1px 0 0 var(--mt-color-border) !important;
	}

	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBContainerContent,
	.mtStatusTabs .sapMITBContent {
		border: none !important;
		box-shadow: none !important;
	}

	/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   12c. Transparent text&icon buttons
	   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   Every toolbar action in the kit is the same component - no fill, no border, a
	   brand-blue glyph beside a brand-blue label - and §3b / §5 / §11 declare that
	   colour from two classes (`.mtTableActions .sapMBtnIcon`). sap.m's own button
	   rule is exactly as specific (`.sapMBtn .sapMBtnIcon`), and in the launchpad
	   the theme is the later stylesheet, so the tie went to the theme and the
	   glyphs came out in its icon colour: איפוס פילטרים, הגדרות תצוגה and
	   ייצוא לאקסל on the reports screen, and תצוגת היררכיה / מיון / הדפסה /
	   פתיחת כל השורות on נתוני כל העובדים.

	   Colours only - the fills are deliberately left alone. §3b / §5 already win
	   the background (three classes against the theme's two), and an !important
	   background here would also outrank the :hover fills those sections declare
	   and kill the hover feedback on every one of these buttons - as well as the
	   white pill of .mtFilterToggle. */
	.mtFilterActions .sapMBtnIcon,
	.mtTableActions .sapMBtnIcon,
	.mtTableToolbar .sapMBtnIcon,
	.aeTableToolbar .sapMBtnIcon,
	.hmPrintButton .sapMBtnIcon,
	.hmMonthArrow .sapMBtnIcon,
	.hmMonthField .sapMBtnIcon,
	.hmCardFooter .sapMBtnIcon,
	.hmPage .hmLauncherCard .sapMBtnIcon {
		color: var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
	}

	.mtFilterActions .sapMBtnContent,
	.mtTableActions .sapMBtnContent,
	.mtTableToolbar .sapMBtnContent,
	.aeTableToolbar .sapMBtnContent,
	.hmCardFooter .sapMBtnContent {
		color: var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
	}

	/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   12d. Row actions - היעדרויות / השתלמויות
	   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   The only icons in the app that never had a colour of their own: both columns
	   hold a bare Transparent button and took the theme's default, which read as
	   brand blue under sap_horizon and as the shell's icon colour in the
	   launchpad. Stated explicitly now, forMoAb both tables at once - the two screens
	   deliberately draw these columns alike (see the note on colAbsences).

	   Scoped through the tables rather than through a class on the buttons, so the
	   views stay as they are; sap.m.Table and sap.ui.table have separate cell DOM,
	   hence the two selectors per rule. The sub-row selector covers the pop-in
	   layout the reports table falls back to on a tablet. */
	.mtRow .sapMListTblCell .sapMBtn .sapMBtnIcon,
	.mtTable .sapMListTblSubCnt .sapMBtn .sapMBtnIcon,
	.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl td.sapUiTableCell .sapMBtn .sapMBtnIcon {
		color: var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
	}

	/* Both 12c and 12d: the glyph itself is drawn by ::before, and a theme that
	   colours it there rather than on the span would otherwise still win. */
	.mtFilterActions .sapMBtnIcon::before,
	.mtTableActions .sapMBtnIcon::before,
	.mtTableToolbar .sapMBtnIcon::before,
	.aeTableToolbar .sapMBtnIcon::before,
	.hmPrintButton .sapMBtnIcon::before,
	.hmMonthArrow .sapMBtnIcon::before,
	.hmMonthField .sapMBtnIcon::before,
	.hmCardFooter .sapMBtnIcon::before,
	.hmPage .hmLauncherCard .sapMBtnIcon::before,
	.mtRow .sapMListTblCell .sapMBtn .sapMBtnIcon::before,
	.mtTable .sapMListTblSubCnt .sapMBtn .sapMBtnIcon::before,
	.aeTable .sapUiTableCtrl td.sapUiTableCell .sapMBtn .sapMBtnIcon::before {
		color: inherit !important;
	}

	/* ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   12e. TreeTable expand / collapse arrow
	   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
	   Same story as 12d - §8d colours it through `.aeTable .sapUiTableTreeIcon`
	   (two classes), which is a tie with sap.ui.table's own rule. */
	.aeTable .sapUiTableTreeIcon,
	.aeTable .sapUiTableTreeIcon::before {
		color: var(--mt-color-brand) !important;
	}
}


/* ============================================================================
   13. שורת ה-header של האפליקציה - the shell bar
   ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
   view/AppHeader.fragment.xml, included once by App.view.xml above the
   sap.m.App. Navy strip, the system name and the way home at the start (right,
   in RTL), the bell and the user circle at the end.

   Two structural notes:

   (1) The bar and the screens are the two rows of one flex column (.shShell).
       The bar keeps its own height and the sap.m.App takes what is left, which
       is what stops a screen's own scrolling from running underneath the bar or
       past the bottom of the window. sap.m.NavContainer sets height:100% on
       itself, so .shContent has to take that back - hence the two-class
       selector, which also settles the tie in the launchpad (§12(1)).

   (2) Colours are stated against .shBar rather than globally: the strip is the
       one dark surface in the app and everything in it is inverted, so scoping
       keeps that inversion from leaking into a screen.
   ========================================================================= */
.shShell {
	height: 100%;
}

/* Both rows are addressed through .shShell so they outrank sap.m's own
   one-class .sapMFlexItem, whose stylesheet is the later one inside the
   launchpad (§12(1)) - a bar that starts shrinking there would be a hard
   thing to trace back to a tie in the cascade. */
.shShell > .shBar {
	flex: 0 0 auto;
}

.shShell > .shContent {
	flex: 1 1 0;
	height: auto;
	min-height: 0;
}

.shBar {
	box-sizing: border-box;
	height: var(--mt-shell-height);
	padding: 0 var(--mt-content-inset);
	background-color: var(--mt-color-navy-deep);
	color: var(--mt-text-inverse);
	font-family: var(--mt-font-sans);
}

/* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
   13a. Right: system name + way home
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
.shBrand > * {
	margin-inline-end: var(--mt-space-2);
}

.shBrand > *:last-child {
	margin-inline-end: 0;
}

.shBar .shSystemTitle,
.shBar .shTitleSeparator {
	color: var(--mt-text-inverse);
	font: var(--mt-text-field-value);
	font-family: var(--mt-font-sans);
}

/* The link is the only interactive text on the strip, so it carries the weight
   the plain system name does not - that difference is what says it can be
   clicked, since an underline only appears on hover. */
.shBar .shHomeLink,
.shBar .shHomeLink:visited {
	color: var(--mt-text-inverse);
	font: var(--mt-text-button);
	font-family: var(--mt-font-sans);
	text-decoration: none;
}

.shBar .shHomeLink:hover,
.shBar .shHomeLink:active {
	color: var(--mt-text-inverse);
	text-decoration: underline;
}

/* Keyboard focus has to be visible on navy, where the theme's dark focus ring
   is not. */
.shBar .shHomeLink:focus,
.shBar .shIconButton:focus,
.shBar .shIconButton:focus .sapMBtnInner {
	outline: var(--mt-border-width) dotted var(--mt-text-inverse);
	outline-offset: 2px;
}

/* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
   13b. Left: notifications + user
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
.shActions > * {
	margin-inline-start: var(--mt-space-4);
}

.shActions > *:first-child {
	margin-inline-start: 0;
}

/* Three classes deep on purpose: sap.m's own `.sapMBtn .sapMBtnIcon` is a tie
   at two, which the launchpad's later stylesheet would win (§12(1)). The glyph
   is drawn by ::before, where a theme may colour it again. */
.shBar .shIconButton.sapMBtn,
.shBar .shIconButton .sapMBtnInner,
.shBar .shIconButton .sapMBtnIcon,
.shBar .shIconButton .sapMBtnIcon::before {
	background: transparent;
	border: none;
	box-shadow: none;
	color: var(--mt-text-inverse);
}

.shBar .shIconButton .sapMBtnIcon {
	font-size: var(--mt-font-size-lg);
}

.shBar .shIconButton:hover .sapMBtnInner,
.shBar .shIconButton .sapMBtnInner:hover {
	background: rgba(255, 255, 255, 0.12);
}

/* The count sits on the bell rather than beside it, so the pair reads as one
   object and the row does not reflow when the number appears or changes width
   (translate keeps it centred on the corner at any width). */
.shNotifications {
	position: relative;
}

.shBar .shBadge {
	position: absolute;
	top: -2px;
	inset-inline-start: -2px;
	min-width: 18px;
	height: 18px;
	box-sizing: border-box;
	padding: 0 5px;
	border-radius: var(--mt-radius-pill);
	background-color: var(--mt-color-white);
	color: var(--mt-color-navy-deep);
	font: var(--mt-font-weight-bold) var(--mt-font-size-xs) / 18px var(--mt-font-sans);
	text-align: center;
	pointer-events: none;
}

.shBar .shAvatar {
	flex: 0 0 auto;
}

/* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
   13c. Narrow screens
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   The gutter only. Everything on the strip is one short phrase and one button,
   which still fits at 320px, so nothing has to be dropped - and the system name
   is the only place the app says what it is.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
@media screen and (max-width: 599px) {

	.shBar {
		padding: 0 var(--mt-space-5);
	}
}

/* ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
   13d. Print
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------
   §10 takes the app's chrome off the page; the strip is chrome.
   ------------------------------------------------------------------------- */
@media print {

	.shBar {
		display: none;
	}
}

/* ============================================================================
   Design tokens - SAP UI5 Attendance System Design System
   ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
   Transcribed from the "מערכת נוכחות" Figma kit via the Claude Design project
   "SAP UI5 Attendance System Design System" (tokens/colors.css, tokens/
   typography.css, tokens/layout.css) plus the exact geometry read off the
   frame components:

     52:4199  מסננים דוחות נוכחות באחריות ממונה   (filter bar)
     57:3979  שורה טבלת דוחות באחריות ממונה        (table row)
     86:3634  טבלת דוחות באחריות ממונה             (table + status tabs)
     57:3552  ויזארד השלמת תהליך                   (process wizard dots)
     87:5507  שדה סינון                            (filter field)
     1:7600   תגית עובד                            (employee/population tag)
     3:483    Button                               (21 variants)

   All tokens are namespaced `--mt-*` so they never collide with UI5's own
   `--sapXxx` theme parameters. Values are the raw Figma values (no rounding);
   px is used wherever the kit specifies px so the screens stay 1:1 with the
   design regardless of UI5's compact/cozy root font size.

   Reuse: import this file first, then any screen stylesheet. Registered in
   manifest.json under sap.ui5/resources/css.
   ========================================================================= */

	   anything heavier. The fallback chain still holds ifMoAb the request fails,
